A `.wlg`'s route runs its function for the request's method, `get`, `post` and so on, called with the request and the response, and awaited; `HEAD` calls `get` when there is no `head`; another method is a 405, and a `.wlg` exporting no method's function a 404.

### 6.13 FastCGI

**The owner's answers** (17 November 2026, "FastCGI: A Host Beside IIS's", M0.19): a host that speaks FastCGI, beside the ASP.NET Core adapter, so that a web server other than IIS, nginx or Apache, runs WASP's sites, as it runs PHP's; run both ways, first as PHP-FPM runs, `wasp fastcgi --root site --port 9000`, a process the server's service manager, systemd or a Windows service, starts and keeps running, which the web server reaches by TCP, or by a Unix socket on Linux, then as Apache's mod_fcgid starts PHP, by the web server itself, on demand, its socket given as its standard input; FastCGI's protocol, a record's header and its kinds, written in WebLingo's own C#, in a new adapter, `WebLingo.Hosting.FastCgi`, on .NET's sockets, with no package, rather than through a package that implements it; and the host tried with nginx and with Apache httpd, beside its tests.

**As built** (M0.19, 17 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "FastCGI"): `WebLingoFastCgiServer`, in `WebLingo.Hosting.FastCgi`, an adapter beside the ASP.NET Core one, whose worker pool, reloading and streaming response it shares, translates FastCGI's responder role to WLHI's and back, and `wasp fastcgi` runs it, listening on a port of the local machine, 9000 unless given another, or on a Unix socket, or on the socket or the named pipe a web server gives it as its standard input. Tried with its tests, which speak FastCGI to it as a web server does, and behind the web servers themselves, on Windows (17 November 2026, `WebServerTests`): nginx 1.30.6, and Apache httpd 2.4.71, Apache Lounge's build, through `mod_proxy_fcgi`, each to `wasp fastcgi` on a port, and through `mod_fcgid` 2.3.10, starting `wasp` itself on a named pipe as its standard input, each configured as the WASP User Manual's section 18 configures it; which found two faults, fixed: nginx sends a request's `Content-Type` and `Content-Length` as headers too, which were read twice, and a connection closed with a request's last `FCGI_STDIN` unread, which came after its response, was reset, losing the response's end. Not yet on Linux.

---

## 7. Platform abstraction layer (WL-P001–WL-P016)

### 7.1 Approved M0.5 decisions

- **WL-P007:** Runtime-owned `WebLingoOperation` semantics, not .NET `Task`/`ValueTask` as the core contract.
- **WL-P004:** Synchronous **and** asynchronous filesystem APIs, with blocking work isolated from VM workers.
- **WL-P013:** Worker-local mutable heaps, immutable shared application data and explicit cross-worker transfers.
- **WL-P016:** Portability inventory and CI enforcement, rather than immediately forbidding every unimplemented Native C# feature.

### 7.2 PAL layers

```text
Portable WebLingo core (lexer, compiler, WLBC, VM, GC)
                           |
                 PAL abstractions
                           |
            +--------------+--------------+
            |                             |
        .NET adapter                  Native adapter
     (.NET 10 initially)        (Native C# RTL / OS APIs)
```

The core must not directly depend on ASP.NET Core, ADO.NET, `System.IO.File`, `HttpClient`, platform P/Invoke or a specific host. Platform services are injected at runtime initialization and capability-filtered for each application.

### 7.3 Proposed platform services interface

```csharp
public interface IWebLingoPlatformServices
{
    IWebLingoFileSystem FileSystem { get; }
    IWebLingoClock Clock { get; }
    IWebLingoSchedulerHost Scheduler { get; }
    IWebLingoNetworkService Network { get; }
    IWebLingoCryptoService Crypto { get; }
    IWebLingoDiagnostics Diagnostics { get; }
}
```

These are **conceptual contracts**, not frozen source-level APIs.

### 7.4 Filesystem (WL-P004)

- Open/read/write/flush/metadata/delete operations, synchronous and asynchronous; whether a path is a folder (`IsFolder`, M0.14.2). Implemented in part (15 November 2026) as `IWebLingoFileSystem`, which `std:fs` reaches (§3.9), its asynchronous forms the synchronous ones run off the worker (`IWebLingoSchedulerHost`, §7.8); bytes, read and written whole or through a handle, and a file copied as it is, from 18 November 2026.
- Portable paths and file metadata.
- Resource-owning file handles with canonical disposal.
- No unbounded blocking on cooperative VM workers.
- Capability-safe path resolution must address traversal, symlinks, Windows drive/UNC paths, case differences, device names and TOCTOU races; authorize opened objects where possible. Built for a site's scripts (17 November 2026, §7.13, "File capabilities").

### 7.5 Buffers (WL-P005)

- Mutable and read-only buffer views with explicit handle, offset and length.
- Owners may be VM heap, request allocation, I/O adapter or native resource.
- Pending operations retain or root buffers until completion.
- No raw managed addresses exposed across portable contracts.

### 7.6 Clocks (WL-P006)

- Wall clock for timestamps.
- Monotonic clock for timeouts and elapsed-time measurements.
- Time-zone/calendar services separate from raw clock primitives.
- Injectable clock for deterministic tests.

**As built** (16 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "Dates and times"): the wall clock is `IWebLingoClock`'s `GetUnixTimeNanoseconds()`, and the server's zone `IWebLingoTimeZone` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`): its offset at an instant, and at a time on its own clocks, `DotNetClock` and `DotNetTimeZone` (`WebLingo.Platform.DotNet`) on .NET; an execution has them as `WebLingoExecutionContext.WallClock` and `TimeZone`, which the command lines and the web host give, and the portable core reads no clock and no zone of .NET's (`PortabilityTests`). The platform's other zones are found through the server's, by their IANA names, its `Find(name)` (§3.2, "Time zones"), `DotNetTimeZone`'s asking .NET's `TimeZoneInfo.TryFindSystemTimeZoneById`.

### 7.7 Asynchronous operations (WL-P007) — approved ownership

Core-visible operations are runtime-owned, with states conceptually:

`Created → Pending → Completed | Failed | Cancelled`

- Exactly one terminal state.
- Adapter completions may arrive on arbitrary threads.
- VM continuation resumes only through the owning worker's queue.
- Cancellation is cooperative and races with completion; terminal-state arbitration must be atomic.
- .NET `Task`/`ValueTask` may be used **inside adapters** but do not define WebLingo execution semantics.
- An optimized production implementation need not allocate a separate interface object for every operation.

### 7.8 Scheduler (WL-P008)

- Post/wake primitives for named VM workers.
- Worker affinity for frames, register storage and mutable heap state.
- Proposed instruction budgets and safe points to prevent starvation.
- Blocking I/O routed to asynchronous APIs or bounded blocking pools.

### 7.9 Networking (WL-P009)

- Capability-aware outbound connections and protocol adapters.
- Authorization must consider hostname, resolved IP, port, protocol, redirects, DNS rebinding and TLS verification.
- HTTP is a higher-level service; transport primitives remain portable.

**The owner's answers** (16 November 2026): an HTTP client, `std:http`, `await http.get(url)` and `await http.post(url, body, headers)`, giving the status, the headers and the body, and sockets, `std:net`, both first; and the hosts a script may reach: any under `weblingo`, and under `wasp` only those its site's `wasp.config` allows, `"network": { "allow": ["api.example.com"] }`, as COM's objects are allowed (§3.10). A host a script may not reach is a `NetworkPermissionError`, WL3017, a class and a code of its own (the owner's answer, 17 November 2026, so that each refusal of the configuration is told apart, in place of WL3001; its name and code Proposed).

**As built** (16 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "Network access"): the platform's network is `IWebLingoNetwork` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`), `DotNetNetwork` on .NET (`WebLingo.Platform.DotNet`), which an execution has as `WebLingoExecutionContext.Network` and native functions as `WebLingoHeap.Network`, and which checks a host against the application's where it makes the connection; `std:http` and `std:net` are native modules installed with the runtime, `std/http.dll` and `std/net.dll`, each operation run off the worker (`WebLingoScheduler.TryRunOffWorker`), a failure a `NetworkError`, WL3014. A host is checked by its name or its address as the script gives it; the addresses it resolves to, DNS rebinding among them, are not checked yet.

### 7.10 Cryptography (WL-P010)

Proposed baseline: secure random bytes, SHA-256/SHA-512, HMAC, constant-time comparisons, password hashing via approved algorithms and TLS through networking adapters. Keys require controlled access and feasible zeroization.

**The owner's answers** (17 November 2026, "Cryptography: `std:crypto`", M0.18): a standard module, `std:crypto`, through the platform layer, so that the portable core reaches no .NET cryptography; passwords hashed by PBKDF2 with HMAC-SHA-256, built into .NET, 600,000 iterations and a 16-byte random salt, as OWASP advises, the result one string that names them, `pbkdf2-sha256$600000$salt$hash`, so that a later default still verifies an older hash, rather than Argon2id or bcrypt through a package; functions, `crypto.randomBytes(n)`, `crypto.sha256(data)`, `crypto.sha512(data)`, `crypto.hmac("sha256", key, data)`, `crypto.equal(a, b)` in constant time, `crypto.hashPassword(password)` and `crypto.verifyPassword(password, hash)`, data a string, as UTF-8, or `Bytes`, a hash given as `Bytes`, rather than Node.js's hashes built in pieces; and `hashPassword` and `verifyPassword` synchronous, each blocking its worker, with `hashPasswordAsync` and `verifyPasswordAsync`, which run off the worker, for the asynchronous forms (the owner's own answer, naming them so), the rest synchronous.

**As built** (17 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "Cryptography"): `std:crypto`, `std/crypto.dll`, through the platform's `IWebLingoCrypto`, `DotNetCrypto` on .NET.

### 7.11 Database providers (WL-P011)

Provider-neutral connections, parameter binding, transactions, cancellation, streaming result sets and deterministic disposal. Initial suggested providers: SQL Server and SQLite via ADO.NET; later native providers and ODBC. Connection pooling belongs to providers/adapters, not the VM.

**Illustrative** WebLingo usage (exact async connection API not yet frozen):

```javascript
using (var db = Database.open("MainDatabase")) {
    var rows = await db.query(
        "SELECT Id, Name FROM Customers WHERE Active = @active",
        { active: true }
    );
}
```

**The owner's answers** (16 November 2026): one standard module for every database, `std:db`, `var db = await Database.open("fish")`, `await db.query(sql, values)` giving an array of maps, one for each row, `await db.execute(sql, values)` the rows changed, `await db.transaction(async () => { … })`, and `using`, as the example above has it, rather than a module for each database; what one database has and another has not reached as JDBC, ADO.NET, PHP's PDO and Go's `database/sql` reach it: the SQL passed on as it is, each database's own, `db.provider` and `db.serverVersion` to choose it by, what is common but tunable an options map, an option a provider does not take an error, and what only one database has a provider's extension module, `std:db/sqlserver`, later; a statement's values named, `@name`, from a map, not by position; a database named in `wasp.config`, `"databases": { "fish": "sqlite:…" }`, a script naming it by its name, `weblingo.config` the same for scripts, and a connection string in a script too, as classic ASP's pages had one; a site's SQLite file outside the site's folder, as the IIS logs are, its path in the configuration; and ADO.NET's providers, Microsoft.Data.Sqlite and Microsoft.Data.SqlClient, in a new adapter, `WebLingo.Data.DotNet`, outside the portable core.

**The owner's answers** (17 November 2026, "DatabaseAccess: MS SQL Server"): SqlClient's native network layer on Windows, Microsoft.Data.SqlClient.SNI.runtime, which Microsoft's own terms let an application distribute only within it, its users agreeing to terms that protect it at least as much, and Microsoft indemnified, is left out: SqlClient uses its managed network layer on Windows too, as it does on Linux, so that everything WebLingo ships stays MIT, provided it works with LocalDB; and the example of a site on SQL Server, `Examples.wasp/example3`, runs on SQL Server Express LocalDB, which its user installs, its database made by a script it holds, `setup.sql`. The SQL Server installed for it being SQL Server 2025's Developer edition, not LocalDB, the owner chose, the same day, that the example and the tests run on the default instance of the machine's own SQL Server, `localhost`, reached by TCP/IP, which its user enables, those editions leaving it off when installed, in place of LocalDB, which nothing has tried since: the managed network layer speaks TCP/IP to a server, never its shared memory.

**The owner's answer** (17 November 2026, "std:db: The Order of a Connection's Operations"): a connection runs its operations one at a time, and those a script starts on it without awaiting them, as `Task.all([db.query(…), db.execute(…)])` starts them, in no defined order, rather than queued in the order they were started; a script awaits each operation whose order matters.

**As built** (16 November 2026, SQL Server's provider 17 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "Database access"): the platform's databases are `IWebLingoDatabases` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`), `DotNetDatabases` on .NET, in `WebLingo.Data.DotNet`, SQLite's provider, `sqlite`, and SQL Server's, `sqlserver`, through SqlClient's managed network layer, which an execution has as `WebLingoExecutionContext.Databases` and native functions as `WebLingoHeap.Databases`; `std:db` is a native module installed with the runtime, `std/db.dll`, each operation run off the worker, its class `Database`, whose `transaction` is written in WebLingo, since it calls the script's function and awaits it (§15.2, "Native classes' methods written in WebLingo"); and a database's own failure is a `DatabaseError`, WL3015.

#### Streaming rows and additional providers

On 19 November 2026, the owner approved `await db.rows(sql, values)` returning a disposable cursor.
`await cursor.next()` returns the next row map or `null` at exhaustion. It uses the same parameter binding and
row conversion as `query`. The existing `query` method continues to return an array.

- **Stream Database Rows.** Use a forward-only reader on the connection. Exhaustion or disposal releases the reader.
  While the reader is open, other statements and transaction operations on the same connection raise WL3001.
  Existing adapters can implement `IWebLingoStreamingDatabaseConnection` to opt in to streaming.
- **Add the MySQL Database Provider.** The owner chose MySQL as the first additional provider. The .NET adapter uses
  MySqlConnector with `mysql:` connection strings. WASP sites use a database name from `wasp.config` so the site
  operator controls driver options that can access local files.
- **Add the PostgreSQL Database Provider.** The owner chose PostgreSQL after MySQL. The .NET adapter uses Npgsql
  with `postgresql:` connection strings. A WASP site opens a name from `wasp.config` so the site operator controls
  driver options that can read local files, including password and certificate files. The adapter binds WebLingo
  dates as UTC instants for PostgreSQL's `timestamptz`.

### 7.12 Resource ownership (WL-P012–P013)

- PAL C# implementations may expose `IWebLingoDisposable.Dispose()`; WebLingo uses lowercase `dispose()`.
- Native C# destructors may implement internal PAL cleanup but do not change WebLingo semantics.
- In-flight I/O retains handles/buffers until completion or safe cancellation.
- Worker-local mutable objects cannot be concurrently mutated by foreign workers.

### 7.13 Capabilities and errors (WL-P014–P015)

- Application generation owns an immutable capability set; request policy may only restrict it.
- Enforce access at actual filesystem/network/database/process/native boundaries.
- Translate host exceptions (e.g., `IOException`) into stable WebLingo error categories/codes with optional cause and diagnostic context.
- Avoid leaking host internals to HTTP clients.

**File capabilities** (the owner's answers, 17 November 2026, "File Capabilities: A Site's Folders", M0.16): under `wasp`, a site's scripts reach only the folders its `wasp.config` lists, as `"network"` lists hosts (§7.9), in two lists, `"files": { "read": ["../data"], "write": ["../uploads"] }`, a folder listed to be written readable too, so that a site that only reads its catalog cannot change it, rather than one list of folders read and written; a site that lists none reaches no file, as a site without `"network"` reaches no host; a relative path in a script, `fs.readText("../data/teas.json")`, is from the site's root, as an include's and the configuration's own paths are, the same under `wasp serve` and IIS, and a listed folder, relative, from the configuration's folder; a path is checked by its real path, made absolute, `.` and `..` resolved and its links and junctions followed, compared as the file system compares names (ignoring case on Windows), Windows' device names (`CON`, `NUL`, `\\.\…`) always refused, rather than by the path as written; a path a site may not reach is a runtime error of a class of its own, `FilePermissionError` (the owner's own answer, in place of WL3001 or an `IOError`, the class named `PermissionError` until the owner had it renamed, the same day, beside `NetworkPermissionError` and `ComPermissionError`, "Permissions: The Folders' Class's Name", nothing kept for the old name); and a SQLite database a page's script names by its own connection string, `Database.open("sqlite:../data/shop.db")`, is checked as `std:fs`'s paths are, for reading and writing, those the configuration names in `"databases"` trusted, the configuration being the site's own. Under `weblingo`, a script reaches any file, as before.

**As built** (17 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "File capabilities"): the platform's `IWebLingoFileCapabilities`, `DotNetFileCapabilities` on .NET, which a site's scripts' file system, and its databases, are given; `FilePermissionError`, WL3016.

### 7.14 Native portability (WL-P016)

- Track C# features and BCL dependencies as **Portable**, **Planned**, **Adapted** or **Prohibited**.
- Reflection.Emit and uncontrolled runtime code generation are prohibited in the portable core.
- ASP.NET Core, ADO.NET, P/Invoke and OS-specific threading are adapter-only.
- Generics, exceptions, delegates and other Native C# features are tracked against actual compiler progress.
- CI should check dependency boundaries and run cross-platform conformance tests.

### 7.15 The machine's environment

**The owner's answers** (17 and 18 November 2026, "Write std/Environment"): a standard module, `std:env`, its class `Environment`, `import { Environment } from "std:env";`, its members static, as `Database.open` is: the machine's name, `Environment.machineName`, an environment variable, `Environment.variable("PATH")`, `null` for one that is not set, and `Environment.variables`, a map of them, rather than a built-in class in every program; under `wasp`, a site reading the machine's name only where its `wasp.config` allows it, and a variable only as the configuration lists it, `"env": { "machineName": true, "variables": ["PATH", "APP_*"] }`, a name ending in `*` allowing those it begins, `"*"` any, nothing without it, rather than one list or all or nothing; anything a site may not read `null`, as for a variable that is not set, so that a script cannot tell them apart, rather than an error; a site's scripts reading only, `Environment.setVariable(name, value)` an error of its own, `EnvironmentPermissionError`, WL3019; and under `weblingo`, a script reaching all of it without a permission, as it reaches any file and any host, and setting variables, for its process alone. The permission is named after the module, `"env"`, and the application's environment, `"environment"`, is renamed `"mode"` (§6.12), nothing kept for the old name.

**The machine's architecture and system** (the owner's answers, 19 November 2026, "The Machine's Architecture and System", at the owner's question whether WebLingo can tell a machine's bitness): `Environment.architecture`, the machine's, and `Environment.processArchitecture`, the running program's, which differ for a 32-bit program on 64-bit Windows, each `"x64"`, `"x86"`, `"arm64"` or `"arm"`, as .NET's runtime identifiers name them, and `Environment.os`, `"windows"`, `"linux"` or `"macos"`; a site reading them only where its `wasp.config` allows them, `"env": { "platform": true }`, `null` otherwise, as it allows the machine's name. As built (19 November 2026, Proposed where it says more): through `IWebLingoEnvironment`'s `Architecture`, `ProcessArchitecture` and `OperatingSystemName`, as .NET's `RuntimeInformation` reports them, another architecture's name in small letters, `"freebsd"` for FreeBSD and `"other"` for another system.

**As built** (18 November 2026; Proposed, §15.2, "The machine's environment"): `std:env`, `std/env.dll`, through the platform's `IWebLingoEnvironment`, `DotNetEnvironment` on .NET.

### 7.16 Standard-library utilities

The owner approved the following order on 19 November 2026 after the path, URL and CSV modules shipped.

- **Add Gzip and ZIP Compression.** `std:compression` works with bytes in memory. `gzip(data)` and
  `gunzip(data, maxBytes)` handle gzip data. `zip(entries)` and `unzip(data, maxBytes)` use a map of file
  names to bytes. The decompression limit covers the aggregate file content, and the heap budget also applies.
  Ignore ZIP directory records and reject duplicate file names. Entry paths are treated as map keys.
- **Add Seeded Random Numbers.** `std:random` exports `Random(seed)`, an independent SplitMix64 generator
  per instance. `next()` uses 53 bits for a number in `[0, 1)`. `nextInt(min, max)` uses rejection sampling
  to give each integer in `[min, max)` equal probability. A seed and the same calls give the same sequence on
  every host. Secure random bytes are available through `std:crypto`.
- **Add CLI Subprocess Execution.** `std:process` runs a local executable off the worker through
  `IWebLingoProcessRunner`. The adapter passes an argument array directly, closes stdin, and reads stdout and
  stderr concurrently. The task returns the exit code and both streams as UTF-8 text. The default deadline is
  30 seconds and the default combined output limit is 1 MiB. A deadline or an output overflow stops capture
  and requests termination of the running child and its process tree. WASP has no subprocess runner.

---

## 8. M0.6 solution scaffolding (WL-S001–WL-S016) — approved

### 8.1 Deliverable and scope

A compilable, minimal **.NET 10 Visual Studio solution**, not a full language implementation. First vertical slice:

`Source → Lexer → Parser → Binder → Lowered IR → Bytecode generator → Verifier → VM → Result`

The first implementation may use an in-memory bytecode representation; WLBC binary serialization comes after encoding decisions are settled.

### 8.2 Proposed repository layout

```text
WebLingo/
  WebLingo.slnx
  Directory.Build.props
  Directory.Packages.props
  global.json
  README.md
  LICENSE
  docs/
    architecture/
    language/
    bytecode/
    hosting/
    portability/
  src/
    WebLingo.Core/
    WebLingo.Language/
    WebLingo.Compiler/
    WebLingo.Bytecode/
    WebLingo.Runtime/
    WebLingo.VM/
    WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions/
    WebLingo.Platform.DotNet/
    WebLingo.Hosting.Abstractions/
    WebLingo.Hosting.AspNetCore/
    WebLingo.Hosting.Standalone/
    WebLingo.StdLib/
    WebLingo.Cli/
  tests/
    WebLingo.Language.Tests/
    WebLingo.Compiler.Tests/
    WebLingo.Bytecode.Tests/
    WebLingo.Runtime.Tests/
    WebLingo.VM.Tests/
    WebLingo.Platform.Tests/
    WebLingo.Conformance.Tests/
  samples/
    HelloWorld/
    Arithmetic/
    Functions/
    SimpleWebsite/
```

Add FastCGI, database provider and native adapter projects when their implementation milestones begin, rather than creating unnecessary empty projects.

**Documents — approved amendment (12 November 2026):** the repository's root holds the solution folder above, `WebLingo/`, beside a flat `Documents/` folder for the project's documents. This specification exists once, as `Documents/weblingo-architecture.md`; there is no `docs/architecture/` folder, and the folder structure is kept shallow: the portability inventory is `Documents/native-compatibility.md`, and there is no `eng/` folder. The whole repository may become public, so nothing that should stay private is put into it.

### 8.3 Project dependencies (WL-S002)

- Core has no WebLingo project dependencies.
- Language depends on core, not compiler/runtime.
- Bytecode depends on core, not compiler/VM.
- Compiler consumes Language and Bytecode.
- Runtime does not depend on Compiler.
- VM consumes Bytecode and Runtime.
- Hosting/CLI compose the engine; framework-specific types do not leak into core.
- All references must form a directed acyclic graph.

### 8.4 Initial contracts (WL-S003–WL-S012)

**Source spans:** stable offsets, lines and columns. Serialized offsets measured in UTF-8 bytes; diagnostics use documented one-based line/column conventions.

**Diagnostics:** severity, stable code, message, source span. Suggested codes:

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| WL1001 | Unexpected token |
| WL1002 | Missing semicolon |
| WL2001 | Undefined identifier |
| WL2002 | Duplicate declaration |
| WL2003 | Assignment to constant |
| WL2004 | Read before initialization |
| WL3001 | Invalid operation |
| WL4001 | Bytecode verification failure |

Codes added while implementing M0.6 (§15.2):

| Code | Meaning | Status |
|---|---|---|
| WL1003 | Invalid character | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1004 | Invalid numeric literal (malformed, or out of range) | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1005 | Unterminated comment | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1006 | Declaration without an initializer | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1007 | Invalid assignment target | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1008 | Source nested too deeply | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1009 | Invalid escape in a string literal | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1010 | String literal not closed on its line | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL1011 | Multi-line string with no closing delimiter | Proposed |
| WL1012 | Multi-line or interpolated string written wrongly: a line not indented as its closing delimiter, a brace where none may stand, more quotes or dollars than it takes, or a format that is empty or whose precision is too large | Proposed |
| WL2005 | Not a function (a call of something that cannot be called) | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL2006 | Wrong number of arguments in a call | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL2007 | `break` or `continue` outside a loop | Proposed |
| WL2008 | `return` outside a function | Proposed |
| WL2009 | `this` outside a method or a constructor | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL2010 | `throw;` alone outside a `catch` block | Proposed |
| WL2011 | A class declaring both `dispose()` and `destructor()` | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL2012 | `break`, `continue` or `return` leaving a `finally` block | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL2013 | A class that extends itself, directly or through others | Proposed |
| WL2014 | `await` outside an `async` function and a script's top level | Proposed |
| WL2015 | A module an import names that cannot be found or read | Proposed |
| WL2016 | A module's parameter that its import does not give, or a value given for one it does not declare | Proposed |
| WL2017 | A warning: a result discarded that its call exists to give, an array's `sort()` or `reverse()`, which give a new array | Proposed |
| WL3002 | Integer overflow (a result outside int64) | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL3003 | Execution budget exceeded (instructions or registers) | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL3004 | Execution cancelled by the host | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL3005 | Index out of range | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL3006 | No such member | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL3007 | No such key | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL3008 | Uncaught exception: a value a program threw, which nothing caught | Approved (13 November 2026) |
| WL3009 | Deadlock: the program waits for a task that nothing can complete | Proposed |
| WL3010 | Timeout: a task that did not complete in the time `Task.timeout` gave it | Proposed |
| WL3011 | I/O failure: a file or a folder that is not there, or cannot be read, written or removed (`std:fs`, `IOError`) | Approved (15 November 2026) |
| WL3012 | COM failure: an `Invoke` that fails, or an object that cannot be made (`windows:com`, `ComError`, §3.10) | Approved (15 November 2026) |
| WL3013 | Division by zero: an integer's remainder by 0 (`DivisionByZeroError`) | Approved (16 November 2026) |
| WL3014 | Network failure: a host that cannot be reached, a connection refused or broken, a request that takes too long (`std:http`, `std:net`, `NetworkError`) | Proposed |
| WL3015 | Database failure: a statement the database refuses, a database that cannot be opened (`std:db`, `DatabaseError`) | Proposed |
| WL3016 | Permission denied: a file or a folder a site's scripts may not reach, outside the folders its `wasp.config` lets it read or write, or a device's; a SQLite database's file likewise (`std:fs`, `std:db`, `FilePermissionError`, §7.13, "File capabilities") | Proposed |
| WL3017 | Network permission denied: a host a script's configuration, its `"network"`, does not let it reach (`std:http`, `std:net`, `NetworkPermissionError`, §7.9) | Proposed |
| WL3018 | COM permission denied: a COM object an application's configuration, its `"com"`, does not let it make or bind to (`windows:com`, `ComPermissionError`, §3.10) | Proposed |
| WL3019 | Environment permission denied: an environment variable a site's scripts try to set, under `wasp`, which lets them read only (`std:env`, `EnvironmentPermissionError`, §7.15) | Approved (18 November 2026) |
| WL3020 | A program stuck awaiting a task `new Task()` made, nothing else able to run, thrown at that `await`, which a catch may catch (`DeadlockError`, §3.8, "Tasks completed by hand") | Proposed |
| WL3022 | What the platform has not: a member of `std:console`'s `Console` that .NET has on Windows alone, used elsewhere (`PlatformError`, §3.9, "The console: `std:console`") | Proposed |
| WL4002 | Program too large for the bytecode (more registers than a function may have) | Approved (12 November 2026) |
| WL5001 | A template's block, `<%`, with no closing `%>` | Proposed |
| WL5002 | A template an include or a layout statement names that cannot be found or read, or that is outside the application's root | Proposed |
| WL5003 | An include, or a layout, that makes a cycle: a template that includes itself, or is its own layout, directly or through others | Proposed |
| WL5004 | Includes beyond their limits: nested more than 64 deep, or reading more than 16M characters in all | Proposed |
| WL5005 | A component that cannot be found or read when it is rendered, a runtime error | Proposed |
| WL5006 | A component rendered, or a layout used, with a value for a parameter it does not declare, or without one it needs: a runtime error for a component, a compile-time one for a layout | Proposed |
| WL5007 | A layout statement where a page cannot have one: a second one, one after the page has done or written something, or one in a template it includes | Proposed |
| WL5008 | A page's HTML that the escaping of its values cannot follow: HTML browsers read in more than one way, a block of code or an `if`'s branches that end elsewhere than where they began, text that moves the HTML in a statement's body without braces, a component rendered or a layout's body written outside HTML's text, a page that ends outside it, a value where a URL's part is not known (§13, "Context-aware escaping") | Proposed |

**Lexer:** identifiers, keywords (`var`, `const`), literals, operators, punctuation, source spans and malformed-input diagnostics.

**Parser:** initial subset of variable declarations, integer literals, identifiers, arithmetic, parentheses and mandatory semicolons; preserve source spans and recover sufficiently to report multiple errors.

**Binder/IR:** resolve lexical symbols, each from its declaration on (§3.1); produce bound tree and lowered IR independent of register/stack VM choice.

**Bytecode generator:** produce an executable in-memory module; serialization deferred until WLBC encoding finalized.

**Verifier:** reject invalid opcodes, operands, branches, constants and uninitialized temporaries.

**Runtime values:** implement null, Boolean, int64 and binary64 first; decimal128 follows without changing approved language semantics.

**VM:** execute verified modules with an execution context containing owner worker, cancellation, instruction budget, resource budget and runtime services. Initial execution may be synchronous.

**CLI:** executable `weblingo`; first working commands `run`, `check`, `--version`; `compile` and `disasm`, and `run` and `check` of compiled bytecode, since M0.13.2 (§5.3, §15.2, "WLBC on the command line"). `weblingo` is strictly the scripts' runner, and `wasp`, WASP's command line, the web handler, which serves applications, locally and under IIS, so that `serve` is `wasp`'s, not `weblingo`'s (the owner's decision, 16 November 2026): `wasp serve`, since M0.14.5 (§15.2, "`wasp serve`").

Example:

```javascript
import { writeLn } from "std:console";
var a = 10;
var b = 20;
var result = a + b;
writeLn(result);
```

Expected output: `30`. `write` and `writeLn` are built-ins the CLI host registers (§15.2), `std:console`'s, which a script imports, since 20 November 2026 (§3.9, "The console: `std:console`").

### 8.5 Build conventions (WL-S013–WL-S014)

- Target framework: `net10.0`.
- Nullable reference types enabled.
- Implicit usings enabled.
- Deterministic builds; warnings treated as errors.
- Central package management; minimal third-party dependencies in portable core.
- Maintain the portability inventory, `Documents/native-compatibility.md`.
- **Testing default:** xUnit on .NET 10, adopted with M0.6 approval.

Proposed central props:

```xml
<Project>
  <PropertyGroup>
    <TargetFramework>net10.0</TargetFramework>
    <Nullable>enable</Nullable>
    <ImplicitUsings>enable</ImplicitUsings>
    <LangVersion>latest</LangVersion>
    <TreatWarningsAsErrors>true</TreatWarningsAsErrors>
    <Deterministic>true</Deterministic>
  </PropertyGroup>
</Project>
```

### 8.6 Initial conformance tests (WL-S015)

| Program / scenario | Expected behavior |
|---|---|
| `var x = 10;` | Valid |
| `const x = 10;` | Valid |
| `const x;` | Compile error |
| `var x = 10` | Missing-semicolon compile error |
| `var x = 1; var x = 2;` | Duplicate declaration error |
| `x = 10;` | Undefined identifier error |
| `var x;` | Compile error |
| `writeLn(x); var x = 1;` | Undefined identifier error |
| `var x = 10 + 20;` | Result 30 |
| `var x = 10 / 2;` | Result 5.0 |
| Signed int64 overflow | Runtime error |
| Malformed source | Diagnostic, not compiler crash |
| Malformed bytecode | Verification failure, not VM crash |

**Note:** every declaration has an initializer and a name means nothing before its declaration (§3.1), so no code but a hoisted function (§3.5) can read a variable before it has a value, and only that read is checked at run time (WL2004).

### 8.7 Implementation increments (WL-S016)

1. **M0.6.1:** solution skeleton, projects, references, CLI shell, test harness.
2. **M0.6.2:** lexer and tokenization tests.
3. **M0.6.3:** parser, AST and diagnostics.
4. **M0.6.4:** binder and lowered IR.
5. **M0.6.5:** minimal bytecode generator and verifier.
6. **M0.6.6:** minimal register VM, arithmetic, locals and a built-in that writes output (`write`, `writeLn`).
7. **M0.6.7:** end-to-end conformance on Windows/Linux.

### 8.8 M0.6 acceptance criteria

- Builds cleanly on Windows and Linux.
- Dependency rules enforced.
- CLI compiles and runs simple `.wlg` programs.
- Lexer, parser, binder, generator, verifier and VM are separate components.
- Tests pass; malformed inputs fail safely.
- Portability inventory exists.
- Portable core has no ASP.NET Core or ADO.NET dependency.
- Compiler IR is not tied to a permanent VM choice.
- Build and test instructions are documented.

---

| Milestone | Purpose | Status |
|---|---|---|

| M0.3 | WLBC bytecode and VM architecture | Drafted; the VMs compared (13 November 2026, §5.6), the choice and several encoding choices open |
| M0.4 | Hosting ABI and runtime lifecycle | **Four explicit architectural decisions approved** |
| M0.5 | Platform abstraction and portability | **Four explicit architectural decisions approved** |
| M0.6 | Solution scaffolding and implementation contracts | **Approved; M0.6.1–M0.6.7 implemented and verified on Windows and Linux (§15)** |
| M0.7 | Conformance suite expansion: the language of §3.5, in parts M0.7.1 to M0.7.6 (below), each with its conformance tests | Implemented (12 November 2026), on Windows; `foreach` waits for collections |
| M0.8 | Windows/Linux CI and portability validation | Started (12 November 2026): the CI workflow builds and tests on Windows and Linux (first run passed: CI run 1, 12 November 2026, commit `5229806`); tests check the portable core's assemblies against the dependency rules (§8.3) and the portability inventory (WL-P016) |
| M0.9 | Collections: arrays, maps and sets (§3.2), with indexing, `length` and member access (§3.3), and `foreach` (§3.5) | Implemented (13 November 2026), in parts M0.9.1 to M0.9.6 (below), on both VMs, on Windows |
| M0.10 | Classes, `dispose()` and `using` (§3.2, §3.4, WL-L011) | Implemented (13 November 2026), in parts M0.10.1 to M0.10.7 (below), on both VMs, on Windows; typed catches, `is` and the classes of values implemented too (13 November 2026), but for extending `Array`, `Map` and `Set` |
| M0.11 | Asynchrony: `async`, `await` and tasks (§3.8) | Implemented (13 November 2026), in parts M0.11.1 and M0.11.2 (below), on both VMs, on Windows |
| M0.12 | `.wasp` templates: includes, layouts and parameterized components, together (§13, WL-T001 to WL-T008) | Its contracts decided in part (§13, "Contracts", 13 November 2026); after M0.11, and the module system it needs (`import` and `param`), both done; in parts M0.12.1 to M0.12.5 (below, 14 November 2026), all implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs, on Windows, `samples/SimpleWebsite` running through `wasp`; what WL-T007 and WL-T008 ask of a host (dependency invalidation, application generations, cancellation and streaming to a response) waits for hosting |
| M0.13 | WLBC, the bytecode's binary format (§5.1, §5.3) | Implemented (16 November 2026), in parts (below), on both VMs: a format for each VM's bytecode, in one container, both VMs kept (15 November 2026); WASP never writing its files, and the format's questions answered (16 November 2026) |
| M0.14 | Hosting: web applications served through the ASP.NET Core adapter, for IIS first (§6, WL-H009), their pages and `.wlg` route handlers compiled and cached in memory, their generations reloaded atomically (WL-T007), and served by `wasp`, locally and under IIS (§8.4) | The owner's choice (16 November 2026); in parts (below), M0.14.1 first |
| M0.15 | Context-aware escaping in pages: a page's `<%= %>` escaped for where it stands in the HTML, an attribute's value, an event's, a URL's, `<script>` and `<style>`, not only for HTML's text, raw output, `<%- %>`, staying explicit (§2.2, open question 9) | The owner's choice (17 November 2026), its design the owner's answer the same day (§13, "Context-aware escaping"); implemented (17 November 2026), on both VMs |
| M0.16 | File capabilities: under `wasp`, a site's pages reaching only the folders its `wasp.config` allows, as `"network"` allows hosts, `std:fs` checking each path where it opens it (§3.9, "Files: `std:fs`", §7.4, §7.13, open question 13) | The owner's choice (17 November 2026), its design the owner's answers the same day (§7.13, "File capabilities"); implemented (17 November 2026) |
| M0.17 | COM objects on Windows: `windows:com`, as §3.10 designs it | The owner's choice (17 November 2026); its main points approved (15 November 2026), the rest 17 November 2026; implemented (17 November 2026), on both VMs, on Windows |
| M0.18 | Cryptography: `std:crypto`, secure random bytes, SHA-256 and SHA-512, HMAC, comparisons in constant time and password hashing, through the platform layer (§7.10) | The owner's choice (17 November 2026), its design the owner's answers the same day (§7.10); implemented (17 November 2026), on both VMs |
| M0.19 | FastCGI: a host beside the ASP.NET Core adapter, translating FastCGI's requests to WLHI's, so that web servers other than IIS run WASP's sites (§6.1, §6.2) | The owner's choice (17 November 2026), its design the owner's answers the same day (§6.13); implemented (17 November 2026), on Windows, and tried behind nginx and Apache httpd there |
| M0.20 | Standard modules, imported: nothing of a standard module in a program that does not import it, `Json`, `Html`, `write`, `writeLn`, `delay`, `Bytes`, `DateTime` and `Regex` among them, the compiler suggesting the import a name needs (§3.9, "The standard library") | The owner's decision (18 November 2026), its number Proposed; in parts (below), all implemented (18 November 2026) |

**M0.7 parts** (12 November 2026), each landing on its own with its conformance tests, which `WebLingo.Conformance.Tests` runs beside §8.6's:

1. **M0.7.1:** blocks and nested scopes. Implemented (12 November 2026); a missing `}` is WL1001, at its `{`.
2. **M0.7.2:** `true`, `false` and `null`; `==`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`; `!`, `&&`, `||`. Implemented (12 November 2026).
3. **M0.7.3:** `if` and `else`. Implemented (12 November 2026); a branch that is not a block still has a scope of its own, so `if (c) var y = 1;` declares `y` for that branch only (proposed).
4. **M0.7.4:** `while`, `for` and `do … while`, with `break` and `continue`. Implemented (12 November 2026); `break` or `continue` outside a loop is WL2007 (proposed), and a `for`'s per-iteration bindings (§3.5) become observable, and are implemented, with closures in M0.7.6.
5. **M0.7.5:** string literals and concatenation. Implemented (12 November 2026), with the first heap objects (§15.2, "Heap").
6. **M0.7.6:** functions, function values and closures. Implemented (12 November 2026).

**M0.10 parts** (13 November 2026), each landing on its own with its conformance tests, on both VMs:

1. **M0.10.1:** classes: declarations, `new`, fields, methods, `this`, methods as values, `x?.name`, and text (§3.7). Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **M0.10.2:** inheritance: `extends`, `super(…)`, `super.name(…)`, and the base's constructor run first. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
3. **M0.10.3:** disposal: `dispose()` and `destructor()`, once and chained, and `using` on its ways out but exceptions. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **M0.10.4:** static members. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
5. **M0.10.5:** `Object` and `equal()`. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
6. **M0.10.6:** exceptions: `throw`, `try`, `catch` and `finally`, `Error`, runtime errors as exceptions, and `using` when one leaves it. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
7. **M0.10.7:** `toString()`. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.

**M0.11 parts** (13 November 2026), each landing on its own with its conformance tests, on both VMs:

1. **M0.11.1:** tasks and `await`: `async` functions, methods and function expressions; `await` of any value, in them and at a script's top level; tasks (`Task`, `x is Task`), their exceptions, those nothing awaits, and `t.forget()`; the end of an execution; `delay(milliseconds)`; and a benchmark workload of asynchronous suspension (§5.6). Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **M0.11.2:** asynchronous disposal: an `async` `dispose()`, which `using` awaits, and WL3001 where it cannot. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.

**Modules** (§3.9, 14 November 2026), in parts, each landing on its own with its conformance tests, on both VMs, before M0.12, which builds on them:

1. **Static imports and exports:** `import db from "lib/db.wlg";` and `import { f, g } from "lib/util.wlg";`, `export`, a module's instance and its exports, read as they are when read, each module run once, its imports first, cycles; the host finding the files; diagnostics and runtime errors naming the file they are in. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **Parameters:** `param`, the values an importer gives, and a missing one's error. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
3. **Dynamic imports:** `await Module.import(path)`, a module compiled when it runs. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **Standard modules:** the scheme `std:`, and `std:system`, which every module imports, with `write` and `writeLn`. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
5. **A script's parameters:** `weblingo run script.wlg name=value`. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.

**Native modules** (§3.9, 14 November 2026), in parts, each landing on its own with its tests, on both VMs:

1. **Native functions:** `IWebLingoNativeModule` and `IWebLingoNativeRegistry`, a module's functions; a host's schemes, from `weblingo.config` and `--scheme`; an assembly loaded from a scheme's folder only, into the host's context; and a native module imported by its scheme, or by a path within a scheme's folder, statically and while the program runs. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **Native classes:** a module's classes, `registry.Class(…)`, their constructors, methods and static members native functions, made by the heap's own methods (§3.7, one implementation of classes). Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
3. **Standard modules as native modules:** `std:system`'s built-ins in a native module installed with the runtime, in `std/` beside its program, the `std` scheme configurable as any other. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **The built-in classes as native classes:** `Array`, `Map`, `Set`, `Task`, `Module`, `Class` and `Function` as `std:core`'s native classes, their methods, properties and indexers native functions, called directly by the VMs and through methods written in WebLingo, both measured against the VMs' own on the benchmark, for the owner to choose on the numbers. Measured (15 November 2026, §5.6); the owner had the built-in classes keep the VMs' own members, and what serves every native module landed: native classes' properties and indexers, the VMs calling native members directly, and arguments read where they are. Implemented (15 November 2026), on both VMs.

**M0.12 parts** (14 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests, on both VMs, the first `.wasp`-capable release having all of them together (WL-T001, WL-T008):

1. **M0.12.1, pages:** `WebLingo.Templates`, a `.wasp`'s text, `<% … %>`, `<%= … %>`, `<%- … %>` and `<%# … %>`, statements across blocks, escaping, `null`, and line breaks (§13, "Contracts"), compiled through the WebLingo compiler into a program that writes the page; `wasp run page.wasp`, in `WebLingo.Wasp.Cli`, writing it to standard output; and `WebLingo.Templates.Tests`. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **M0.12.2, includes:** `<% include "/includes/header.wasp"; %>`, textual, resolved and compiled with the page, and a cycle of them an error of the templates' range (WL5001 on). Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
3. **M0.12.3, components:** `param` in a component, `renderComponent(path, values)`, a task, the component found and compiled when it runs, as `Module.import(…)` finds and compiles a module (§3.9), and a missing one's error. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **M0.12.4, layouts:** `<% layout "/layouts/main.wasp", { title: … }; %>`, `renderBody()`, a layout's own layout, and a cycle of them. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
5. **M0.12.5, template output's benchmark workload** (§5.6), the third of the workloads "Both VMs, for now" waits for. Implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs, and measured (§5.6, "Template output").

**M0.13 parts** (16 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests, on both VMs:

1. **M0.13.1, the format:** WLBC's writer and reader for both VMs' modules (§5.3, "As built"), each section's CRC-32 checked, a damaged file or one of an unknown version rejected as WL4001, and what is read verified; every conformance program written, read back and run on both VMs. Implemented (16 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **M0.13.2, the command line:** `weblingo compile [--register|--stack] script.wlg`, writing `script.wlbc`; `weblingo run script.wlbc`, on the VM its header names; `weblingo disasm script.wlbc`, with a disassembler of the stack bytecode; the native modules a `.wlbc` names loaded when it runs; its files named from the program's folder; and `weblingo run script.wlg` writing no `.wlbc`. Implemented (16 November 2026), on both VMs.

**M0.20 parts** (18 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests, building the owner's decision (§3.9, "The standard library"):

1. **M0.20.1, the import taught:** a name a standard module exports, used without importing it, WL2001, `'Database' is not defined; did you mean Database, from std:db?`, and a name differing from one in scope only by case, `did you mean count?`, so that each name the later parts take out of every program's scope teaches its import from then on. Implemented (18 November 2026).
2. **M0.20.2, `std:json` and `std:html`:** `Json` and `Html` only by an import, a page's own escaping keeping its use of them. Implemented (18 November 2026).
3. **M0.20.3, `std:io`:** `write` and `writeLn` only by an import, a template's text and its `<%= %>` writing without one, every script, sample, test, example, workload and document gaining its import. Implemented (18 November 2026); `std:system`, imported implicitly still for `delay`, until part 4.
4. **M0.20.4, the rest:** the classes of the kinds of values no literal makes, each a module's, `Bytes` and `BytesBuilder` `std:bytes`'s, `DateTime` `std:datetime`'s and `Regex` `std:regex`'s, imported to name the class, a value of the kind keeping its members without; and `delay` `std:system`'s, with `write` and `writeLn` moved back to it, `std:io` gone, `std:system` imported implicitly by every program, an import of its names allowed and meaning the same (A3); every script, sample, test, example, workload and document gaining its imports, as the owner answered (18 November 2026). Implemented (18 November 2026).

**Standard classes extended, parts** (18 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests, on both VMs, building the owner's answer (§3.7, "No sealed classes"):

1. **Their values' methods:** an instance of a class extending a kind of value's class, `String`, `Integer`, `Number`, `Boolean`, `Decimal`, `Regex` or `Function`, has its value's methods, as it has its properties, each giving what the value's own gives: `new Name("anna").toUpper()` is `"ANNA"`, and `new Strict("a+").test("aa")` is `true`. Implemented (18 November 2026), on both VMs: a call `TryFindCallee` does not find on such an instance goes to its value's member, as a native class's fallback does (`WebLingoMembers.TryCall`).
2. **`Bytes`, `BytesBuilder`, `DateTime`, `Json` and `Html`:** no longer sealed; a class extending one of the first three holds a value of its kind, made by `new` as §3.7 says, and `new Bytes(…)` and `new DateTime(…)` make plain values; a class extending `Json` or `Html` reaches their static members, `MyJson.parse(text)`. Implemented (18 November 2026), on both VMs (§15.2, "Classes holding a value").
3. **`Task` and `Module`:** no longer sealed, `new` taking what the owner answered (§3.7, "No sealed classes"). `Module`'s implemented (18 November 2026), on both VMs: `new Module(name)` and a class extending `Module`, its instances modules with an instance's fields and class (`WebLingoHeap.MarkModuleClass`), `exec()` `Module`'s method, written in WebLingo. `Task`'s implemented the same day, after "Tasks completed by hand" (§3.8), on both VMs: a class extending `Task` makes tasks completed by hand, each with an instance's fields and class, its class's methods found before a task's own (`WebLingoMembers.TryGetValueMethod`), written `Job:task`; such a task does not record where `new` made it, so that a program stuck on one is told only where it awaits it, `new` on any class not being slowed to record it.
4. **The standard modules' classes:** a class extending one, `class LogFile extends File {}`, getting its instances as the owner answered (§3.7, "No sealed classes"): created by `new`, then opened by its instance methods, its factories shorthand for both. Implemented (18 November 2026), on both VMs: `File`'s `open` and `openSync`, `Socket`'s `connect` and `Database`'s `open`, its name given to `new Database(name)`, each giving the instance; a factory written in WebLingo, `static async open(this, …) { var made = new this(); await made.open(…); return made; }`, a static method taking the class it is called on as its receiver, which a static method's lookup binds to that class, the verifiers allowing a static method a receiver; an instance opened once, and not after its disposal, its methods saying it is not open until it is.
5. **Held values taken as their kind's:** wherever a built-in takes a value of a kind, a string, an integer, bytes, a date or a regular expression, it takes an instance holding one, as that value, `"ab".startsWith(new Name("a"))`, `fs.readText(new Path("a.txt"))`, `[1, 2][new Index(1)]`, as a class written in WebLingo's instance is taken where its base's is; through the argument checks that name the argument, whose messages say what each argument must be, which it shares. Implemented (18 November 2026), on both VMs: a string's, bytes', a date's, a regular expression's, a map's and a set's methods, an array's that take a position or a separator, the built-in classes' methods, indices and keys, a native module's functions and a native class's members, and the elements `Bytes.concat`, `Bytes.from` and `join` read, each given a copy of its arguments with each held value as its kind's where any is held (`WebLingoHeap.TryUnwrapHeld`); what keeps what it is given keeps the instance, `push`, `insert`'s value, a task's `complete`, `setConstant`, and what writes it, `write` and `writeLn`, writes the instance.

**Console module, parts** (20 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests, building the owner's answers (§3.9, "The console: `std:console`"):

1. **The writing and the reading:** `std:console`, a native module installed with the runtime as `std/console.dll`: `write`, `writeLn`, `writeError` and `writeErrorLn` (A9), and the console's reading, `readLine`, `readLineSync`, `prompt` and `readPassword`, moved from `std:system` (A1), which keeps `delay`, a program having them only by importing them; under `wasp`, `std:console` not installed, its import WL2015 (A3); a page's blocks writing with `write` and `writeLn` of their own, and a `.wlg` route handler with `response.write` and `response.writeLn` (A2); every script, sample, test, example, workload and document gaining its import. Implemented (20 November 2026), on both VMs, neither of which changed (§15.2, "The console").
2. **The console's control:** `Console`, its members static, all of .NET's `Console`, the writing and the reading among them (A4), `readKey()` and `readKeySync()` giving a map (A6), and `ConsoleColor`, the colours, which the colour members take as their names too (A5). Implemented (20 November 2026), on both VMs, neither of which changed, with the owner's answers to its questions (the register, "The console's control"; §15.2, "The console").
3. **Console programs on Windows:** a script a console program only when it imports `std:console`: `weblingo.exe`'s manifest's `detached`, a console made where such a script has none, and `weblingow.exe`, a Windows application (A7); a failure without a console shown in a message box (A8). Implemented (20 November 2026), neither VM changed (§15.2, "The console").
4. **A double-click runs `weblingow.exe`:** `.wlg` files registered to `weblingow.exe` (A7), by `Scripts/add-wlg-file-type.wlg` and the Windows setup, the uninstaller removing it, an earlier registration of `weblingo.exe` moved over. Implemented (20 November 2026): the registration's keys as before, `WebLingo.Script` and `Applications\weblingo.exe`, so that a choice made of `weblingo.exe` holds, their command and icon `weblingow.exe`'s; the uninstaller removing a registration of either (§15.2, "The console").

**M0.14 parts** (16 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests:

1. **M0.14.1, WLHI's HTTP contracts** (§6.7): a request's method, path, query, scheme, host, headers and body, and a response's status, headers and body, Uncommitted, then Committed, its status and headers fixed, then Completed; and a request and a response held in memory, for tests and for hosts that buffer. Implemented (16 November 2026).
2. **M0.14.2, the host's core** (§6.3 to §6.6): the runtime, its applications and their generations, an application's pages and handlers compiled and cached in memory (§5.1), a request's path found among them, run on a worker, its output written to the response, and a runtime error's response. Implemented (16 November 2026).
3. **M0.14.3, the request and the response in scripts:** what a page and a `.wlg` route handler see of them, and what a handler is. Implemented (16 November 2026).
4. **M0.14.4, the ASP.NET Core adapter** (§6.1, WL-H009): ASP.NET Core's requests translated to WLHI's, an application's other files served as they are, and IIS through the ASP.NET Core Module, which runs `wasp`. Implemented (16 November 2026), its IIS deployment, `wasp serve` named in a site's `web.config`, coming with M0.14.5.
5. **M0.14.5, serving an application locally** on Kestrel, with `wasp serve` (§8.4; `weblingo` runs scripts only); and an example application, `Examples.wasp/example1` (`Examples/example1` until 18 November 2026) at the repository's root, a site's own files, its pages beside its `web.config`, which an IIS server runs with WebLingo installed once for all its sites (§6.9; the owner's requests, 16 November 2026). Implemented (16 November 2026).
6. **M0.14.6, reloading** (§6.5, WL-T007): an application's files watched, a new generation compiled when one changes, and published atomically once healthy, the old one drained. Implemented (16 November 2026).
7. **M0.14.7, streaming** (§6.7, WL-T008): a response's body written as the page runs, with backpressure, and a request's body read as it arrives, neither buffered whole. In parts of its own (below).

**M0.14.7 parts** (16 November 2026), each landing on its own with its tests, building the owner's answers (§6.12, "Streaming"):

1. **M0.14.7.1, output sent as it is written:** a page's output kept until it reaches 64 KB, then its response committed and its output sent as it is written, in pieces; a runtime error after that ending the response unfinished, its error in the host's log and, in development, at the page's end; and the ASP.NET Core adapter sending a response's body as it comes, rather than once the page has run. Until part 3, a write the client has not yet taken keeps the worker waiting. Implemented (16 November 2026).
2. **M0.14.7.2, executions that wait without a thread** (§6.6): an execution waiting only for work off its worker returns to its host, kept, and goes on where it was once that work is posted back, on both VMs; a host's worker runs other requests' executions meanwhile. Implemented (16 November 2026), on both VMs.
3. **M0.14.7.3, backpressure:** a page whose output the client has not yet taken waits as at an `await`, its worker running other requests meanwhile, template output an asynchronous operation (WL-T008), for at most a time, its request then cancelled. Implemented (16 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **M0.14.7.4, a request's body as it arrives:** `await request.text()` and `await request.form()` (the owner's answer, 16 November 2026, §6.12), and the ASP.NET Core adapter giving the body as it comes, rather than read whole before the page runs. Implemented (16 November 2026).
5. **M0.14.7.5, a body's bytes:** `await request.read()`, the body's next piece as bytes as it arrives (the owner's answer, 16 November 2026, §6.12), and the value that holds bytes (§3.2, "Bytes"), which the language does not have yet, designed first (added 16 November 2026). Implemented (16 November 2026), on both VMs.

**Extending the classes of values** (§3.7, 13 November 2026), in parts, each landing on its own with its conformance tests, on both VMs:

1. **`extend` blocks:** methods and static members given to a class, a program's or a kind of value's (`extend Integer { … }`), hoisted with its block's classes, reaching every instance and subclass, made before or after, a method replacing one of the same name. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **Classed collections' text:** `Stack([1, 2])`, in place of `Stack [1, 2]`. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs; replaced by part 6's.
3. **Classes extending `Boolean`, `Integer`, `Number` and `String`:** instances holding a value, given by `super(value)`, which arithmetic, comparisons and `==` use, arithmetic's result keeping a class, written `Celsius:21.5`; and `new` on those classes. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **`Function`:** `new Function(f)`, `new Function()`, and classes extending it, whose instances are functions with methods of their own. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
5. **`Class`** (designed 14 November 2026, §3.7), in parts of its own, each landing on its own with its conformance tests, on both VMs: (5.1) making classes: `new Class(…)`, functions with a receiver, and `setMethod`, a constructor included, implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs; (5.2) names: `__className`, `__instanceOf` and `__parent`, implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs; (5.3) properties and constants given while the program runs, `setProperty`, `setStaticProperty`, `setConstant` and `static const`, and assignment replacing a class's static method, implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs; (5.4) `extends Class`, implemented (14 November 2026), on both VMs.
6. **Classed values' text:** `Stack:[1, 2]`, in place of `Stack([1, 2])`. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs, for collections, and with part 3 for the other classed values.
7. **Operators:** `static operator +(a, b) { … }`, and the other operators a class may declare. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.

**M0.9 parts** (13 November 2026), each landing on its own with its conformance tests, on both VMs (§5.6, "Both VMs, for now"):

1. **M0.9.1:** member access (`x.name`, `x.name(…)`) and indexing (`x[i]`, `x[i] = v`), with strings' `length` and indexing (§3.6). Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
2. **M0.9.2:** arrays: literals, indexing, `length`, `push` and `pop`, identity and text. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
3. **M0.9.3:** maps: literals, entries, `has`, `remove`, `length`, keys and text. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
4. **M0.9.4:** `foreach` over arrays, strings and maps, and a benchmark workload of collections. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
5. **M0.9.5:** sets: literals, `add`, `has`, `remove`, `length`, `foreach` and text. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.
6. **M0.9.6:** optional access, `x?[k]`. Implemented (13 November 2026), on both VMs.

**After M0.8** (order approved by the owner, 13 November 2026): M0.9, then M0.10, M0.11, M0.12 and M0.13, as the table gives them (asynchrony put before templates on 13 November 2026, the owner asking for it to be designed first). Each needs syntax, or a format, that this specification does not give yet, which is decided feature by feature before it is built; and each is built on both VMs (§5.6, "Both VMs, for now").

**Immediate next activity:** M0.14, hosting (the owner's choice, 16 November 2026), in parts (below), its design answered (§6.12): M0.14.1, WLHI's HTTP contracts, M0.14.2, the host's core, M0.14.3, the request and the response in scripts, M0.14.4, the ASP.NET Core adapter, M0.14.5, `wasp serve` and the example application, M0.14.6, reloading, and M0.14.7, streaming, in its five parts, are implemented, and the configuration is `wasp.config` for a site, `weblingo.config` for a script: M0.14 is implemented (16 November 2026). What comes after it is the owner's choice (17 November 2026): M0.15, context-aware escaping in pages, implemented (17 November 2026), then M0.16, file capabilities, implemented (17 November 2026), M0.17, COM objects on Windows, implemented (17 November 2026), M0.18, cryptography, implemented (17 November 2026), and M0.19, FastCGI, implemented (17 November 2026), as the table gives them, each designed before it is built, on both VMs where it reaches them. M0.13, WLBC, is implemented (16 November 2026). The compiler and both VMs cover M0.6's subset, the language of §3.5 to §3.9, and pages, their includes, components and layouts (§13, M0.12).

---

## 10. Open questions and specification risks

These items should be explicitly tracked rather than silently treated as approved:

1. **Register versus stack VM (WL-BC002):** compare equivalent benchmark workloads; register-based remains preferred but provisional. Compared on 13 November 2026 (§5.6, "The comparison"): the register VM is faster on every workload measured, the stack bytecode smaller. The owner kept both VMs, each feature built on both, until property access, async suspension, template output and I/O can be measured too (§5.6, "Both VMs, for now"); every one of them measured, both still, on 15 November 2026, until something more is.
2. **WLBC encoding:** finalize variable-length operand scheme, branch addressing, header/directory layout and binary compatibility. Built (16 November 2026, M0.13, §5.3): branch addressing by instruction index, and a CRC-32 for each section, the owner's decisions; the operands, the layout and compatibility Proposed, nothing frozen until the work is done.
3. **Decimal128 binary encoding:** for now, decimals are .NET's `decimal` and a WLBC file holds a decimal constant as its literal's digits (the owner's answer, 16 November 2026, §3.2); for decimal128, later: choose IEEE BID or alternative representation, define canonicalization, rounding and interoperability.
4. **Exceptions and cleanup:** freeze table-driven versus handler-stack mechanics; define guaranteed disposal chaining and cleanup errors. Decided 13 November 2026 (§3.7): table-driven regions, and an exception from `dispose()` replaces one already on its way out; and a disposal goes on when one of its methods throws (approved 13 November 2026, §3.7).
5. **Async disposal:** specify `using` behavior around async operations, cancellation and outstanding native resources. `using` awaits an `async` `dispose()` (§3.8, approved 13 November 2026, and implemented in M0.11.2); cancellation and native resources remain.
6. **`WebLingoValue` layout:** prove 16-byte representation, GC roots and handle validity across both toolchains.
7. **Cross-worker data:** formalize immutable sharing, serialization, ownership transfer and lifetime rules.
8. **Application generation unloading:** define retained task lifetimes, draining deadlines and leak handling.
9. **Template escaping:** define context-aware escaping and safety rules for raw output. Decided 17 November 2026 (§13, "Context-aware escaping"): a value escaped for where it stands, as Go's `html/template` escapes it, with functions for what the compiler cannot tell; raw output stays explicit, `<%- %>`, the author's to make safe.
10. **Native ABI:** finalize versioning, calling convention, callback threading, ownership and error structures.
11. **Database API:** settle synchronous versus asynchronous connection establishment, transactions and streaming rows.
12. **Native C# readiness:** track compiler/RTL capabilities and identify adapter replacements in CI.
13. **Security:** specify concrete capability resolution and sandbox limitations; logical isolation is not a security boundary equivalent to separate processes. A site's files decided and built (17 November 2026, §7.13, "File capabilities").
14. **Scheduler fairness:** choose instruction quantum, cancellation safe points and bounded blocking pools.
15. **Initial test infrastructure:** xUnit adopted as the M0.6 default; implementation and CI configuration remain to be generated.

---

## 11. Guiding architectural invariants

1. **One language semantics across hosts and toolchains.** WebLingo behavior must not change when replacing .NET adapters with native adapters.
2. **No .NET dependency in the final native product.** The .NET 10 stage is a bootstrap and delivery strategy, not a language requirement.
3. **Portable source and bytecode.** WLBC must be validated before execution and must not expose host memory structures.
4. **Clear ownership boundaries.** Worker-affine mutable heaps, explicit cross-worker transfer, deterministic resource disposal.
5. **Separation of disposal and GC.** `dispose()` is deterministic resource release; tracing GC reclaims script-object memory.
6. **Stable host-neutral interfaces.** IIS, FastCGI and standalone hosts adapt to WLHI (§6.2), not the reverse.
7. **Safe publication.** Hot reload swaps whole initialized application generations atomically.
8. **Secure by default.** Escaped template output, capability-aware services and bounded resource use.
9. **First-class template composition.** Includes, layouts, and parameterized components must all ship with the first `.wasp` template-capable release; no phased deferral.
9. **Measure before freezing VM design.** Keep the lowered IR VM-neutral and benchmark register versus stack execution.
10. **Incremental vertical slices.** Establish a working compiler/VM pipeline before expanding language and hosting complexity.

---

## 12. WL-G001 — Open-Source Distribution and Governance (proposed)

**Decision status:** WebLingo is intended to be free and open source. The public-release milestone, release scope, and MIT License remain proposals.

### 12.1 Release objective and timing

- **Proposed public-release milestone:** A functioning, independently deployable native WebLingo compiler/runtime that does not require .NET, Node.js, V8, PHP, or the separate Native C# compiler on end-user systems.
- Continue developing in C#/.NET 10 according to the approved bootstrap architecture. The repository may remain private during implementation.
- The independent Native C# compiler is a build-time toolchain, **not** a required component of the WebLingo runtime distribution; whether its own source code is published is a separate decision.
- Publish language semantics, WLBC bytecode documentation, host interfaces, build instructions, and conformance tests sufficient for independent evaluation and interoperability.

### 12.2 Candidate license: MIT

**MIT is the preferred candidate under consideration, not yet selected.** Subject to rights and third-party-license review, MIT would generally permit commercial and noncommercial use, redistribution, modification, embedding in proprietary products, and proprietary forks, while requiring preservation of copyright and license notices in copies or substantial portions of the licensed software. MIT does **not** require downstream applications or modifications to be open-sourced.

Tradeoff: MIT does not require third parties to contribute improvements back. If reciprocal publication of changes becomes a goal, reconsider alternatives such as MPL 2.0 before the public release. MIT also does not expressly grant trademark rights; project branding and names must be handled separately.

**Unresolved license scope:** Determine whether MIT would cover **all** WebLingo components—language compiler, VM, runtime, standard library, IIS/ASP.NET Core and FastCGI adapters, CLI, native adapters, tests, samples, and documentation—or whether any components remain under different terms. Specify documentation licensing explicitly. No assumption is made that the separate Native C# compiler is part of the release.

### 12.3 Open-source readiness requirements (proposed)

1. **Ownership and provenance:** Track copyright holders, contributors, third-party code, assets, and generated content; verify that the project has the rights necessary to publish each component.
2. **Dependency review:** Maintain a software bill of materials or equivalent dependency inventory; verify redistribution compatibility, required notices, and transitive dependencies. Avoid introducing restrictive dependencies into the portable core.
3. **Reproducible releases:** Provide versioned source tags, documented builds, release artifacts, checksums, and automated Windows/Linux conformance tests.
4. **Specification and compatibility:** Publish versioned language, WLBC, PAL/WLHI and extension contracts; state compatibility and deprecation policies.
5. **Community governance:** Establish contribution rules, code review and maintainer authority, a security disclosure process, and a code of conduct before accepting substantial public contributions.
6. **Trademark and naming:** Conduct a fresh name/trademark and ecosystem-confusion check for **WebLingo** before public launch; the MIT software license does not establish availability of the name or rights to a logo.
7. **Security:** Document the limitations of capability-based logical isolation and the need for OS-level process isolation in hostile multi-tenant deployments.

### 12.4 Proposed public repository files

```text
LICENSE                 # Standard MIT text, if MIT is finally approved
README.md
CONTRIBUTING.md
CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md
SECURITY.md
NOTICE.md                # Only if needed for third-party notices
THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md   # If required by dependencies
CHANGELOG.md
 docs/                   # Versioned language and runtime specifications
 src/
 tests/
 samples/
```

The actual `LICENSE` file must identify the correct copyright holder and year(s). Do not present MIT as the project's operative license until the owner expressly selects it and the repository is licensed accordingly.

### 12.5 Questions reserved for later approval

- Confirm **MIT versus another license**.
- Decide the **components covered** and documentation license.
- Decide **public repository timing**, governance, and contribution licensing (including whether a CLA or DCO is needed).
- Clear the **WebLingo** name and determine whether a trademark/brand policy is appropriate.
- Define the **native-release readiness criteria** and whether preview source releases occur earlier.

---

## 13. Template composition and reuse (WL-T001–WL-T008) — APPROVED

**Decision:** The first implementation that supports `.wasp` pages **MUST provide all three mechanisms together**: template includes, layouts, and parameterized components. Earlier suggestions to implement them gradually are superseded. Named sections remain a proposed extension, not a requirement approved by this decision.

### WL-T001 — Unified composition model

A `.wasp` file is a server-side template compiled through the common WebLingo compiler and executed by the same VM as `.wlg` code. Composition does **not** introduce a second template interpreter. Three constructs are first-class:

- **Include:** embeds a reusable template fragment, usually a header, footer, or navigation region.
- **Layout:** supplies a shared page shell and a designated body insertion point.
- **Parameterized component:** renders a reusable template fragment using explicitly passed parameters in an isolated scope.

These are template composition constructs, not independent languages or client-side frameworks.

### WL-T002 — Includes (required at initial template release)

Illustrative proposed syntax:

```html
<!-- index.wasp -->
<% include "/includes/header.wasp"; %>
<main><h1>Welcome</h1></main>
<% include "/includes/footer.wasp"; %>
```

An include is resolved at compilation/linking time where feasible and participates in the template dependency graph. Paths are application-root-relative by default, subject to capability checks. The precise syntax and variable-scope rules for includes remain implementation-contract details to finalize; the **feature itself is approved and mandatory**.

### WL-T003 — Layouts (required at initial template release)

A layout defines shared document structure and a content insertion point. Illustrative syntax:

```html
<!-- /layouts/main.wasp -->
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html><head><title><%= pageTitle %></title></head>
<body>
    <% include "/includes/header.wasp"; %>
    <main><% renderBody(); %></main>
    <% include "/includes/footer.wasp"; %>
</body></html>
```

```html
<!-- products.wasp -->
<% layout "/layouts/main.wasp"; %>
<% const pageTitle = "Products"; %>
<h1>Products</h1>
```

A layout may itself use a parent layout if the implementation defines an unambiguous acyclic resolution order. Layout body insertion must not require unconditional whole-page buffering. Exact `pageTitle`/layout-data binding semantics are still to be specified.

### WL-T004 — Parameterized components (required at initial template release)

Components are independently compiled templates with **explicitly supplied parameters and isolated local scopes**. They must not implicitly capture arbitrary caller-local variables.

Illustrative component:

```html
<!-- /components/product-card.wasp; parameter: product -->
<article class="product-card">
    <h3><%= product.name %></h3>
    <p><%= product.description %></p>
</article>
```

Illustrative caller:

```html
<%
    for (const product of products) {
        await renderComponent(
            "/components/product-card.wasp",
            { product: product }
        );
    }
%>
```

The exact component parameter declaration syntax and whether `renderComponent` is a built-in function or compiler-recognized intrinsic remain to be finalized. Compiled components must be reusable without reparsing for every invocation.

### WL-T005 — Escaping and output safety

`<%= expression %>` escapes output by default, including within includes, layouts, and components. `<%- expression %>` is an explicitly unsafe/raw-output mechanism and must not be silently used by composition features. The compiler/runtime must specify context-aware handling of HTML text, attributes, URLs, scripts, and styles before claiming complete injection safety; it does since M0.15 (§13, "Context-aware escaping"). A component must not implicitly convert untrusted strings into trusted HTML. Output remains subject to the same response commitment and streaming rules as ordinary `.wasp` rendering.

### WL-T006 — Resolution, cycles, and security

- Resolve template references within configured application roots and filesystem capabilities; reject traversal and unauthorized access.
