Ctrl+C: `onCancelKeyPress(handler)`, written in WebLingo, watches the presses, each handler its generation, and runs a loop that awaits the next press, a task the platform completes (`WebLingoScheduler.TryWaitForHost`), calls the handler with it on the worker, awaiting a task it gives, and ends the program as the press would have, Windows's `STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT` or 130 elsewhere, where it gives anything but `true`; a press while the handler is still busy with an earlier one, or before the script awaits, ends the program, as .NET's default does; the loop's task pending, the program still ends when its top level does. `Console`'s static methods written in WebLingo reach their class as `this`, `static write(value) { this.__write(…); }`, as any static method does (§3.7, "Static members"). From part 4 (20 November 2026): `Scripts/add-wlg-file-type.wlg` registers `.wlg` files under the keys it wrote before, `WebLingo.Script` and `Applications\weblingo.exe`, which Windows's "open with" choice offers as `weblingo.exe`, so that a choice made of it holds, their command `"…\weblingow.exe" "%1" %*` and their icon `weblingow.exe`'s; the uninstaller (`Installers/Windows/uninstall.ps1`) removes a registration whose command runs either program, and keeps an installation from being removed while either runs from it | Proposed |
| Native classes | From native classes (§3.9; §9, "Native modules", part 2): `registry.Class(name)` gives a `WebLingoNativeClass` (`WebLingo.Runtime`), on which the module declares its constructor, its methods and its static methods, each a native function with an arity, `DeclareConstructor`, `DeclareMethod` and `DeclareStaticMethod`; a class's name, and a member's, are names a program writes, a class's unique among the module's functions and classes, a member's among its kind, a static method and a method sharing one as a class's may, and no member is named `constructor` or `destructor` (its disposal is `dispose`). The compiler makes a native class a class as any other, extending `Object`, bound and made with the built-in classes where the program begins, under a name no program can write, and exported by the module's instance; each member a method whose body calls its built-in, which the host registers as `scheme:module.Class.constructor`, `.name` and `.static.name`, a constructor and a method given `this` first, so that a native class is extended, given methods by `extend` and `setMethod`, disposed by `using`, written and compared as a class written in WebLingo is, and a call of a native method is a call of its member, then of its built-in, the VMs' calls unchanged. Its constructor marks an instance as the class's own, a class without one getting one that only marks; a method given an instance its constructor did not mark, one whose class's constructor a program replaced included, is WL3001, without running. `TrySetHostObject`, `TryGetHostObject<T>` and `TryReleaseHostObject` reach an instance's host object, each checking that the class's constructor marked the instance (WL3001 otherwise); an instance whose host object was released reports "This Counter has been disposed.", WL3001, until the proposed `ResourceClosedError` (§3.4) has a code; a release gives the object to the module, which closes it, and releasing again gives nothing; and a host object still held when the collector frees its instance, or when the execution ends, is disposed if it is `IDisposable`, its failure ignored. A runtime error raised in a native class's member is reported where the member was called, in the file that called it: the member's function is native (`WebLingoFunction.IsNative`), and each VM's failure path looks through it (`RaisedAt`); an error that cannot be caught, a limit of the execution or a cancellation, raised in one is reported where it is, at the start of the program, a member having no place in a file, so that the VMs' failure path for it stays as it was, a change to it having slowed the stack VM's calls. A native `dispose` runs synchronously; an asynchronous one, and a native class extending another, are not built yet. A native class's static property (`DeclareStaticProperty`, 18 November 2026, for `std:env`'s `Environment.machineName`) is a getter, given nothing, and a setter, given the value, or none, each a built-in, `.static.get.name` and `.static.set.name`, which the binder makes the class's static methods `get:name` and `set:name`, as a declared class's static property is made; a name a static method has, or another static property, fails the module | Proposed |
| Files (`std:fs`) | From `std:fs` (§3.9, 15 November 2026): `WebLingoFsModule` (`WebLingo.StdLib.Fs`), installed as `std/fs.dll`; each function has its `Sync` form, `readTextSync`, `writeTextSync`, …, and `File` its `openSync`, `readSync` and `writeSync`. `readText(path, encoding)` gives the file's text, `writeText` and `appendText(path, text, encoding)` write it, making the file, and give `null`; `exists` gives a Boolean, for a file or a folder; `remove` removes a file, one that is not there an `IOError`; `list` gives the names of a folder's files and folders, without their paths, in ordinal order; `makeFolder` makes a folder and the folders above it, one there already not an error; `removeFolder` removes an empty folder, one that is not an `IOError` ("it is not empty"). `File.open(path, mode, encoding)`, a factory ("Native modules' optional parameters and factories"), opens a file to read it, `"r"`, to write it anew, `"w"`, or to append to it, `"a"` (WL3001 for anything else), giving a `File`; `f.read()` gives its next line, without its line break, or `null` at its end; `f.write(text)` writes text, giving `null`; `f.dispose()` closes it, once, `using` disposing it as any instance, and a file disposed, or opened to read and written, or the reverse, is WL3001 and an `IOError` respectively; a file not disposed is closed when the collector frees it or the execution ends. A path is a non-empty string without `\0`, from the process's current folder, or absolute; an encoding is named as .NET names it (`utf-8`, `utf-16`, `utf-16BE`, `utf-32`, `us-ascii`, `iso-8859-1`), `null` for UTF-8, one there is not WL3001; UTF-8 is written without a byte order mark, and read skipping one, another encoding skipping its own; text not valid in its encoding is an `IOError`. An `IOError`'s message is the path as the script gave it, then why, in words that name no path (`'data.txt' cannot be read: there is no such file or folder.`); an argument of the wrong kind is WL3001. The platform's side is `IWebLingoFileSystem` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`), synchronous, its failures `WebLingoFileSystemException`s of a portable kind, and `DotNetFileSystem` on .NET (`WebLingo.Platform.DotNet`). An asynchronous form runs the synchronous one off the worker (`WebLingoScheduler.TryRunOffWorker`), on the host's threads (`IWebLingoSchedulerHost.RunBlocking`, .NET's thread pool on .NET), its outcome posted back (`Post`) and turned into its task's result on the worker, when nothing else can run, the worker waiting for it 100 milliseconds at a time, so that its VM looks at the cancellation signal between waits, or until the next timer; an execution that ends leaves the operations still running to finish, their results dropped. A host gives an execution its file system and its scheduler's side (`WebLingoExecutionContext.FileSystem`, `SchedulerHost`), which the command lines give; without them, `std:fs` is WL3001. From 18 November 2026, bytes, folders and copies: `readBytes(path)` gives a whole file's bytes, `writeBytes(path, bytes)` writes them anew and `appendBytes(path, bytes)` after its bytes, making the file, each giving `null`, `bytes` a `Bytes` (WL3001 otherwise); `isFolder(path)` gives a Boolean, `false` for a file or nothing; `copy(source, target)` copies a file as it is, replacing a file at `target`, and gives `null`, a folder at `source` an `IOError` ("it is a folder, and only a file is copied"), its errors naming both paths as the script gave them (`'a.bin' cannot be copied to 'b.bin': there is no such file or folder.`), a site's capabilities reading `source` and writing `target`. `File.open(path, "rb")`, `"wb"` and `"ab"` open a file for its bytes, to read them, to write them anew, or to write them after its own, without an encoding (WL3001 for one given), `"wb"` and `"ab"` making a file that is not there; such a file's `read(count)` gives its next bytes, as many as `count`, an integer from 1 (WL3001 otherwise), at most 16 MiB (`WebLingoFsModule.MostBytesRead`), so that a large count makes no large buffer, fewer only at its end, and `null` there, and `write(bytes)` writes bytes; a file opened for its text whose `read` is given a count is WL3001, as is a file opened for its bytes read without one, or written text, or the reverse. The platform's side gained `ReadBytes`, `WriteBytes`, `Copy`, and `IWebLingoFile`'s `ReadBytes(count)` and `WriteBytes(bytes)`, and `WebLingoFileMode`'s `ReadBytes` and `WriteBytes` | Proposed |
| File capabilities | From M0.16 (§7.13, "File capabilities"): the platform's `IWebLingoFileCapabilities` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`), `Resolve(path, write)`, a script's path's real path if the site may reach it, else a `WebLingoFileSystemException` of a new kind, `NotAllowed`, its reason naming no path; on .NET `DotNetFileCapabilities`: the path made absolute from the site's root, its `.` and `..` resolved as written, then, from its root, each part that is there and is a link or a junction replaced by its final target (`FileSystemInfo.ResolveLinkTarget`), the parts after the first that is not there kept as they are; the folders it lists resolved so once, when the site loads; compared ignoring case on Windows and macOS, ordinally elsewhere, a path being a folder or within one; a Windows device's path (`\\.\`, `\\?\`, `\??\`), or a device's name as one of its parts, with or without an extension (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM0`–`COM9`, `LPT0`–`LPT9`, `CONIN$`, `CONOUT$`), refused, but only on Windows; a reading, a listing and a look (`exists`, `IsFolder`) needing a folder to read or write, a writing, a removal and a folder's making needing one to write. `DotNetFileSystem`, given them, opens the real path, not the script's, so that what is checked is what is opened; a link made between the check and the opening is not guarded against, no script being able to make one. A site's scripts reach files through a file system of their own (`WebLingoApplicationOptions.ScriptFileSystem`), the host's own reading its pages, includes, components and modules as before. `wasp serve`'s root is the site's, `wasp run`'s the `--root`, or the page's folder; a listed folder is from the configuration's folder, `wasp.config`'s; `"files"` may give `"read"`, `"write"`, or both, each a list of folders, anything else stopping the site from starting. A path refused is a `FilePermissionError`, WL3016, a new code, `'path' cannot be read: it is not in a folder this site may read.`, `… this site may reach no file, its configuration allowing no folder.`, or `… it names a device.`; a link that cannot be followed, a cycle of them, is one too. A SQLite database a script names by its own connection string, under capabilities: its path resolved so, from the site's root, opened to be written only in a folder the site may write, read-only (`Mode=ReadOnly`) in one it may read; `:memory:` and `Mode=Memory` as they are; a `file:` URI refused; a database the configuration names trusted, as before. SQL Server's `AttachDbFilename`, which the server's own process reads, is not checked. A host `"network"` does not allow is a `NetworkPermissionError`, WL3017, and a COM object `"com"` does not list a `ComPermissionError`, WL3018, each a class and a code of its own (17 November 2026) | Proposed |
| COM objects | From M0.17 (§3.10): `windows:com` is `WebLingo.Windows.Com`, installed as `windows/com.dll` beside the command lines, the `windows` scheme's folder by default on Windows only, so that elsewhere `import com from "windows:com"` is WL2015, as for a module that is not installed. `ComObject` and `ComEnumerator` are its native classes, which its functions make (`WebLingoNativeClass.DeclareMadeByModule`, `TryMakeOwn`: the program tells such a class its class value where it begins, a call of a built-in after the built-in classes, `WebLingoBoundProgram.MadeFirst`), and `ComObject` declares a fallback (`DeclareFallback`), its getter, setter and call, which the runtime runs where an instance has no member of the name, a field, a method, a property or a static one, its class's or a base's, in `WebLingoMembers.TryGet`, `TrySet` and, for a call, `TryCall`, to which each VM's method call goes when `TryFindCallee` fails so (`WebLingoMembers.Fallback`), its one change to either VM, in its failure's path. A member's number, from `GetIDsOfNames`, is kept for the object, by its name ignoring case; a name the object does not have is a `MemberError` (WL3006), so that `x?.name` is `null`. A read is `Invoke` with `DISPATCH_PROPERTYGET | DISPATCH_METHOD`, a call the same with its arguments, an assignment `DISPATCH_PROPERTYPUT`, or `DISPATCH_PROPERTYPUTREF` for an object, the other tried if the object will not take it, its value named `DISPID_PROPERTYPUT`; `x[k]` is `DISPID_VALUE` with `k`, or, if the default member takes no argument, its own default member's with `k`, as VBScript reads `rs("Name")`. Every call is made with the invariant locale (0x007F). A failure is a `ComError`, WL3012: its `number` the `HRESULT`, or the `EXCEPINFO`'s `scode`; its `source` and `description` the `EXCEPINFO`'s, or the thread's error information (`GetErrorInfo`, cleared before each call), "" where COM gave none, its message then saying it in its own words, `'Add' failed: the COM object failed (0x800A01C9).`. Coming back, `VT_BYREF` is read as what it points to, `VT_ERROR` as its code, but `DISP_E_PARAMNOTFOUND` as `null`, a type it does not read is WL3001, and an object without `IDispatch` WL3001; a `SAFEARRAY`'s indices are given in its dimensions' order, the first dimension's first, which `SafeArrayGetElement` takes, whatever its documentation says. A date going out is its instant on the server's clocks (`IWebLingoTimeZone`), coming back a date in the server's zone at that time. `com.get(moniker)` needs the moniker itself, or `"*"`, in `"com"`'s list, as a ProgID does for `create`, what `"com"` does not list being a `ComPermissionError`, WL3018; a CLSID is compared as written. A thread enters COM once, `CoInitializeEx` in a single-threaded apartment; `weblingo`'s and `wasp`'s `Main` are `[STAThread]`, and the web host's workers are started so on Windows (`WebLingoWorkerPool`). Left out, as designed: events, arguments by reference and named arguments | Proposed |
| Cryptography | From M0.18 (§7.10): `std:crypto` is `WebLingo.StdLib.Crypto`, installed as `std/crypto.dll`, in the portable core, which reaches the platform's `IWebLingoCrypto` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`; §7.3's conceptual `IWebLingoCryptoService`), `DotNetCrypto` on .NET (`WebLingo.Platform.DotNet`), which an execution has as `WebLingoExecutionContext.Crypto` and native functions as `WebLingoHeap.Crypto`, the command lines and the web host giving it, its functions WL3001 without it; the portable core uses no `System.Security.Cryptography` (`PortabilityTests`). `randomBytes(n)` gives 0 to 1,048,576 bytes; `sha256`, `sha512` and `hmac(algorithm, key, data)`, `"sha256"` or `"sha512"`, give `Bytes`; `equal(a, b)` compares in a time that depends on the lengths only, two of different lengths unequal; data, a key and a password are a string, as UTF-8, or `Bytes`, anything else WL3001. A password's hash is `pbkdf2-sha256$600000$salt$hash`, its salt 16 random bytes and its hash 32, each in RFC 4648's Base64 with its padding, which the module writes and reads itself; `verifyPassword` takes the iterations, from 1 to 10,000,000, the salt and the hash's length from the hash, and compares in constant time; a hash it does not read is WL3001, its text not repeated in the message. The synchronous forms block the worker; `hashPasswordAsync` and `verifyPasswordAsync` run theirs off the worker (`WebLingoScheduler.TryRunOffWorker`), each a task | Proposed |
| The machine's environment | From 18 November 2026 (§7.15): `std:env` is `WebLingo.StdLib.Env`, installed as `std/env.dll`, in the portable core, its class `Environment` a native class whose members are static, two of them static properties, which reaches the platform's `IWebLingoEnvironment` (`WebLingo.Platform.Abstractions`), `DotNetEnvironment` on .NET (`WebLingo.Platform.DotNet`), which an execution has as `WebLingoExecutionContext.Environment` and native functions as `WebLingoHeap.Environment`, the command lines and the web hosts giving it, its members WL3001 without it; the portable core uses no `System.Environment` (`PortabilityTests`). `DotNetEnvironment` is given all of the environment, `weblingo`'s, or what `wasp.config`'s `"env"` allows, `wasp`'s: `"machineName"`, `true` or `false`, and `"variables"`, a list of names, each a name, a name ending in `*` and holding no other, or `"*"`, compared as the platform compares variables' names, ignoring case on Windows; anything else in `"env"` stops the site, as any wrong setting does. `variables` is a map ordered by name, as the platform names them; a name that is empty, or holds `=` or U+0000, is read as `null`. `setVariable(name, value)` sets a variable by .NET's `Environment.SetEnvironmentVariable`, `null` removing it and `""` keeping it, empty, as .NET 10 does; a name that is not a string, a name to set that is empty or holds `=` or U+0000, and a value that is neither a string nor `null` or holds U+0000, are WL3001; under `wasp` it is `EnvironmentPermissionError`, WL3019, `This application may not set the environment variable 'NAME'.` | Proposed |
| FastCGI | From M0.19 (§6.13): `wasp fastcgi [--root <folder>] [--port <port> \| --socket <path> \| --stdin] [--scheme <name>=<folder> …]` listens on 127.0.0.1's port, 9000 unless given another, as PHP-FPM does, or on a Unix socket made at its path, one there before removed, and removed when it stops; or, with `--stdin`, or without it where neither `--port` nor `--socket` is given and its standard input is a listening socket (FastCGI's `FCGI_LISTENSOCK_FILENO`) or, on Windows, a named pipe's server end, as a web server that starts it gives it, serves that socket or pipe, writing its log to its standard error, since a web server reads nothing from its standard output; on a pipe, one connection at a time, the pipe disconnected after each, once the web server has read what was written, and waited on again, as php-cgi does. Its environment is production unless `wasp.config` names another; it reloads the application when its files change, as `wasp serve` does. Only the responder role: another's `FCGI_BEGIN_REQUEST` is answered `FCGI_UNKNOWN_ROLE`; one request at a time on a connection, a second one's `FCGI_CANT_MPX_CONN`; `FCGI_GET_VALUES` answered with `FCGI_MAX_CONNS` and `FCGI_MAX_REQS` 1024 and `FCGI_MPXS_CONNS` 0; a record of an unknown type with `FCGI_UNKNOWN_TYPE`; a request's parameters at most 1 MiB, more closing its connection. A request runs once its parameters have come, its body read as `FCGI_STDIN` brings it; on a named pipe, whose handle a web server may have made for synchronous use, which cannot be read and written at once, once its body has come whole, nothing more read until it is answered, as php-cgi reads one. CGI's variables are translated: its method from `REQUEST_METHOD`; its path from `REQUEST_URI`, before any `?`, decoded, or else `SCRIPT_NAME` and `PATH_INFO`; its query from `QUERY_STRING`, or else `REQUEST_URI`'s after `?`; its scheme `https` where `HTTPS` is `on` or `1`, else `REQUEST_SCHEME`'s, else `http`; its host `HTTP_HOST`'s, else `SERVER_NAME`'s; its headers every `HTTP_` variable, `HTTP_ACCEPT_LANGUAGE` as `Accept-Language`, and `CONTENT_TYPE` and `CONTENT_LENGTH`, nginx's copies of them as headers, `HTTP_CONTENT_TYPE` and `HTTP_CONTENT_LENGTH`, dropped. Its response goes in `FCGI_STDOUT` records of at most 65,535 bytes: CGI's `Status: 200 OK` and its headers, its `Content-Length` when its body is whole, then its body as the page writes it, through the ASP.NET Core adapter's streaming response (`WebLingoStreamingResponse`, given the host's `IWebLingoResponseSink`), so that it is kept, sent, and waited for as under IIS ("Output sent as it is written"); then an empty `FCGI_STDOUT` and `FCGI_END_REQUEST`, `FCGI_REQUEST_COMPLETE`. A file the application leaves to its host is sent as it is, with the content type its extension gives, one with none a 404, as `wasp serve` sends one. A connection is closed after its request unless the web server asked to keep it (`FCGI_KEEP_CONN`): a socket for writing only, once what was written has gone, what the web server still sends read and dropped until it closes the connection too, for at most 30 seconds, as PHP-FPM closes one, since a socket closed whole with input unread is reset, and the response's end may be lost; a request whose web server sends `FCGI_ABORT_REQUEST`, or closes its connection, is cancelled, as one whose client goes is; and a page that ends its response unfinished closes its connection, so that the web server's client sees it unfinished | Proposed |
| Native modules' optional parameters and factories | From `std:fs` (§3.9, 15 November 2026): a native function, or a native class's method or static method, may have optional last parameters (`registry.Function(name, arity, optional, f)`, `DeclareMethod(name, arity, optional, f)`), which its built-in is given as `null` when a call leaves them out, the compiler calling it through a function written in WebLingo whose parameters have those defaults, made where the program begins, after the built-in classes, for a module's function; and a native class may declare a factory (`DeclareFactory`), a static method whose built-in is given its class first, with which it makes the class's instances, marked as its constructor marks them, without running it (`WebLingoNativeClass.TryMake`) | Proposed |
| Multi-line and interpolated strings | From multi-line strings (§3.5): a raw string's opening is three or more quotes of one kind, a run of two being an empty string; with anything but spaces and tabs after it on its line it is a single-line raw string, `"""a "b" c"""`, closed on that line (WL1010 otherwise), and else a multi-line one, closed by the first line whose first other character begins a run of as many quotes, any of the line after them being the program's; a run of more quotes than opened it is WL1012. Its lines between are its text, each without the closing line's indentation, which must begin it (WL1012 otherwise), a line of only spaces and tabs being empty; one never closed is WL1011, at its opening. `$` before a string's quotes interpolates it: a single-line interpolated string takes one `$` (WL1012 otherwise), an ordinary string's escapes and `\{` and `\}`, and a lone `}` is WL1012. An interpolation's code is read by a lexer of its own, from its opening braces to a `}`, or a `:`, outside its own brackets, on the line it began (WL1010 otherwise; an interpolation in a multi-line string does not span lines), and parsed as one expression (WL1001 for none, or anything after it but an alignment, "Interpolation formats and alignments"); interpolations nest at most 32 deep (WL1008). In a raw interpolated string with n `$`, a run of fewer than n braces is text, one of n to 2n − 1 `{` that many less n braces then an interpolation, closed by n `}`; any longer run of `{`, and a run of n or more `}` outside an interpolation, are WL1012. The parser reads an interpolated string into the syntax a program could write but for a class's name, its parts joined by `+`, each value written by `<text>.of(value)`, a class the binder makes as it makes the built-in ones, written in WebLingo, `class Text { static of(value) { if (value == null) { return ""; } return writtenText(value); } }`, under a name no program can write (`WebLingoParser.InterpolationText`), so that nothing after the parser tells an interpolated string apart, and a runtime error in a value's `toString()` is reported as one in that class's source is. `writtenText`, which only that class has, is an instruction of both VMs, `ToText` as written (its C, or on the stack VM its A, 1), which builds a value's text as `write` builds it, calling an instance's `toString()` as `write` calls it, and making one that gives anything but a string WL3001, with `write`'s message, where the interpolation is (17 November 2026; until then, `of` gave what `value.toString()` gave, unchecked, so that `$"{x}"` gave an integer where `toString()` did, and beside text the error was `+`'s; and it called a `toString()` an `extend` block gives a kind of value, which `write` does not). In a template's block, a multi-line string never closed also names the block's `<%` | Proposed |
| Interpolation formats and alignments | From interpolation formats (§3.5): an interpolation's code is its value, then, after a comma at its depth, its alignment, any expression, evaluated after the value, which must give an integer (WL3001 otherwise) of at most 999,999 either way (WL3001 otherwise, `WebLingoInterpolation.MaxWidth`), and nothing after them (WL1001). A format is the text after the `:` to the closing braces, as it stands, without escapes; an empty one is WL1012, and so is a standard one, a letter and digits, whose precision is above 999 (`WebLingoInterpolation.MaxPrecision`), so that no format makes a huge string. A value's text is padded in Unicode scalar values, as `length` counts them (§3.3), and one already as wide is left as it is. `null` with a format writes nothing, as it does without one, and is padded as `""` is; an instance of a class extending `Integer` or `Number` is formatted as the value it holds; a format .NET rejects for the value (`{1.5:X}`) is WL3001, where the interpolation is. The parser reads an interpolation with an alignment or a format as a call of the hidden class's built-in method, a name no program can write, `<text>.<format>(value, width, format)`, the value's text, by `<text>.of(value)`, standing for the value when there is no format, and the runtime runs it (`WebLingoInterpolationFormat`), so that neither VM's dispatch changes | Proposed |
| Layouts | From M0.12.4 (§13, "Contracts"): `layout` is not a keyword. In a `<% … %>` block's code, the name `layout` followed by a string literal is a layout statement, `layout "path";` or `layout "path", { name: value, … };`, its values a map literal, whose keys are the layout's parameters (WL1001 for anything else after the path, WL1002 for a missing `;`). It stands at most once in a page's own text (a page, a component or a layout), after its parameters and imports and before anything else it does or writes, text included but for lines that write nothing; elsewhere, a second one, or one in an included template, is WL5007. The rest of the page is its body: the page imports its layout, a template of its own scope, found by the templates' resolver as an include is, giving it the values, evaluated as an import's, and the body, as an `async` function of the page's scope, so that the body's variables are that function's, and `export` there is WL1001. A layout is read as a page is, but for its `renderBody()`, which awaits the body, wherever it stands in the layout's own text or in a template the layout includes, as often as it is called (none, the body unwritten); `renderBody` anywhere else is a name like any other, and a page's is WL2001. Elsewhere, an import whose path begins `layout:` is a layout's (`WebLingoTemplateParser.LayoutScheme`), which the template compiler's resolver sends to the templates' resolver, the host's resolver finding the page's other imports as a script's, and its parse reads as a layout (`WebLingoCompilerOptions.Parse`, which a host may set to read the files a program imports). The template parser follows the chain of layouts a page leads to when it reads the page's statement: a layout that cannot be found is WL5002, a cycle WL5003, naming the files in it, and a chain longer than 64 WL5004; and it checks the values against the layout's `param` statements, a key that is not one of them, or a parameter without a default left out, being WL5006, at compile time. A layout does not see the page's variables, nor the page the layout's; it may have a layout of its own, its own body then holding its `renderBody()`. As a module, a layout runs once for each set of values, which the body, a new function, makes each run; and its compile-time and runtime errors name it, at its own line and column (`WebLingoTemplateSources`) | Proposed |
| Diagnostic codes | WL1003 to WL1008, WL3002 to WL3004, and WL4002 (§8.4); WL2005 and WL2006 for invalid calls, and WL3001 at compile time too, for a built-in named other than to call it; WL2004 is reported only at run time, when a hoisted function reads a variable before its declaration has run (§3.5) | Approved (12 November 2026) |
